### Q57. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\left(\mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+3} \mid \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)=-1.66 \mathrm{~V}$.
What is potential of $\mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})} \rightarrow \mathrm{Al}^{+3}(0.1 \mathrm{M})+3 \overline{\mathrm{e}}$ at 298 K ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+1.540 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-1.540 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $+1.679 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-1.679 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $+1.679 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given standard reduction potential is $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{red}} = -1.66 \mathrm{~V}$.

The standard oxidation potential is $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{ox}} = -E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{red}} = +1.66 \mathrm{~V}$.

The given half-cell reaction is an oxidation reaction:

$\mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})} \rightarrow \mathrm{Al}^{+3}(0.1 \mathrm{M}) + 3 \mathrm{e}^{-}$

Here, the number of electrons involved is $n = 3$ and the concentration of $\mathrm{Al}^{+3}$ is $0.1 \mathrm{M}$.

Using the Nernst equation for the oxidation potential:

$E_{\mathrm{ox}} = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{ox}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{n} \log_{10}[\mathrm{Al}^{+3}]$

Substituting the values:

$E_{\mathrm{ox}} = 1.66 - \dfrac{0.0592}{3} \log_{10}(0.1)$

$E_{\mathrm{ox}} = 1.66 - 0.0197 \times (-1)$

$E_{\mathrm{ox}} = 1.66 + 0.0197 = +1.6797 \mathrm{~V}$

Rounding to three decimal places, we get $+1.679 \mathrm{~V}$.

Answer: $+1.679 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

---

### Q58. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\left(\mathrm{Fe}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} \mid \mathrm{Fe}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)=-0.44 \mathrm{~V}$ and $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\left(\mathrm{Sn}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} \mid \operatorname{Sn}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)=-0.14 \mathrm{~V}$. What is standard emf of cell containing the two electrodes?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+0.30 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-0.30 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $+0.58 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-0.58 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $+0.30 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given standard reduction potentials:

$\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Fe}^{+2} \mid \mathrm{Fe}} = -0.44 \mathrm{~V}$

$\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Sn}^{+2} \mid \mathrm{Sn}} = -0.14 \mathrm{~V}$

The electrode with the higher standard reduction potential acts as the cathode, while the one with the lower standard reduction potential acts as the anode.

Since $-0.14 \mathrm{~V} > -0.44 \mathrm{~V}$, the tin ($\mathrm{Sn}$) electrode is the cathode and the iron ($\mathrm{Fe}$) electrode is the anode.

The standard emf of the cell is given by:

$\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = \mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\text{cathode}} - \mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\text{anode}}$

$\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = -0.14 \mathrm{~V} - (-0.44 \mathrm{~V})$

$\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = -0.14 \mathrm{~V} + 0.44 \mathrm{~V} = +0.30 \mathrm{~V}$

Answer: $+0.30 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

---

### Q59. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\left(\mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{aq)})}^{+2} \mid \mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)=-2.37 \mathrm{~V}$.
What is potential for $\mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{s})} \rightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{+2}(0.01 \mathrm{M})+2 \overline{\mathrm{e}}$ at 298 K ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+2.3108 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-2.3108 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $+2.4292 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-2.4292 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $+2.4292 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given standard reduction potential is $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{red}} = -2.37 \mathrm{~V}$.

The standard oxidation potential is $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{ox}} = -\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{red}} = +2.37 \mathrm{~V}$.

The given reaction is an oxidation half-reaction:
$\mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{s})} \rightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{+2}(0.01 \mathrm{M}) + 2 \mathrm{e}^{-}$

Here, the number of electrons transferred is $n = 2$ and the concentration of $\mathrm{Mg}^{+2}$ is $0.01 \mathrm{M} = 10^{-2} \mathrm{M}$.

Using the Nernst equation for the oxidation potential at $298 \mathrm{~K}$:
$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{ox}} = \mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\mathrm{ox}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{n} \log_{10} [\mathrm{Mg}^{+2}]$

Substituting the values:
$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{ox}} = 2.37 - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10} (10^{-2})$

$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{ox}} = 2.37 - 0.0296 \times (-2)$

$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{ox}} = 2.37 + 0.0592 = +2.4292 \mathrm{~V}$

Answer: $+2.4292 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q60. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If standard reduction potential $\left(\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\right)$ of $\left(\stackrel{+2}{\mathrm{Ni}_{(\mathrm{aq})}} \mid \mathrm{Ni}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)$ and $\left(\underset{(\mathrm{aq})}{\stackrel{+3}{\mathrm{Al}}} \mid \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)$ are -0.25 V and -1.66 V respectively. What is standard emf of cell reaction $2 \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})}+3 \mathrm{Ni}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+3}+3 \mathrm{Ni}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+2.57 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-2.57 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $+1.41 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-1.91 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $+1.41 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given cell reaction is $2 \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})}+3 \mathrm{Ni}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+3}+3 \mathrm{Ni}_{(\mathrm{s})}$.

In this reaction, oxidation of $\mathrm{Al}$ takes place, so it acts as the anode. Reduction of $\mathrm{Ni}^{+2}$ takes place, so it acts as the cathode.

The standard emf of the cell is calculated using the formula:

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cathode}} - E^{\circ}_{\text{anode}}$

Substituting the given standard reduction potentials:

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Ni}^{+2}/\mathrm{Ni}} - E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Al}^{+3}/\mathrm{Al}}$

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = -0.25 - (-1.66)$

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = -0.25 + 1.66 = +1.41 \text{ V}$

Answer: $+1.41 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q61. MHT CET 2025 (22 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which of the following reactions exhibit minimum standard reduction potential?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 \mathrm{H}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+2 \overline{\mathrm{e}} \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{F}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}+2 \overline{\mathrm{e}} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{~F}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{-}$
- **(C)** $\operatorname{Li}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+\overline{\mathrm{e}} \rightarrow \operatorname{Li}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}+2 \overline{\mathrm{e}} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Cl}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{-}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\operatorname{Li}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+\overline{\mathrm{e}} \rightarrow \operatorname{Li}_{(\mathrm{s})}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard reduction potential values for the given half-reactions are:

$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{H}^{+}/\mathrm{H}_{2}} = 0.00 \mathrm{~V}$

$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{F}_{2}/\mathrm{F}^{-}} = +2.87 \mathrm{~V}$

$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Li}^{+}/\mathrm{Li}} = -3.05 \mathrm{~V}$

$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Cl}_{2}/\mathrm{Cl}^{-}} = +1.36 \mathrm{~V}$

Lithium has the lowest standard reduction potential in the electrochemical series. Thus, the reduction of $\mathrm{Li}^{+}$ to $\mathrm{Li}$ exhibits the minimum standard reduction potential.

Answer: $\operatorname{Li}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+\overline{\mathrm{e}} \rightarrow \operatorname{Li}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

</details>

### Q62. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

For the cell reaction,
$2 \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})}+3 \mathrm{Cu}^{+2}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Al}^{+3}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+3 \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

If $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-1158 \mathrm{~kJ}$, What is $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 3 V
- **(B)** 2.5 V
- **(C)** 2 V
- **(D)** 1.5 V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 2 V**

**Detailed Solution:**

The relationship between standard Gibbs free energy change and standard cell potential is given by:

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -n F E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}}$

For the given cell reaction, the oxidation half-reaction is $2 \mathrm{Al} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Al}^{+3} + 6 \mathrm{e}^{-}$, so the number of moles of electrons transferred is $n = 6$.

Given:
$\Delta G^{\circ} = -1158 \text{ kJ} = -1158000 \text{ J}$
$F = 96500 \text{ C mol}^{-1}$

Substituting the values into the equation:

$-1158000 = -6 \times 96500 \times E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}}$

$-1158000 = -579000 \times E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}}$

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = \dfrac{1158000}{579000} = 2 \text{ V}$

Answer: 2 V

</details>

---

### Q63. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Which of the following relations about $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ cell is false?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\mathrm{E}_{(\text {cathode })}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{(\text {anode })}^{\circ}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \times \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}}{\mathrm{nF}}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {(right) }}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{\text {(left) }}^{\circ}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}}{\mathrm{nF}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard Gibbs free energy change is related to the standard cell potential by the equation $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ} = -\mathrm{nF} \mathrm{E}_{\text{cell}}^{\circ}$.

Rearranging this equation gives $\mathrm{E}_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = -\dfrac{\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}}{\mathrm{nF}}$.

The relation given in the option is missing the negative sign, making it false.

The other relations are correct standard formulas:
$\mathrm{E}_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = \mathrm{E}_{\text{cathode}}^{\circ} - \mathrm{E}_{\text{anode}}^{\circ}$
$\mathrm{E}_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = \mathrm{E}_{\text{right}}^{\circ} - \mathrm{E}_{\text{left}}^{\circ}$
$\mathrm{E}_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = \dfrac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log_{10} \mathrm{K}$ (at $298 \text{ K}$)

Answer: $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}}{\mathrm{nF}}$

</details>

---

### Q64. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\left(\mathrm{Ag}^{+}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})} \mid \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)=+0.80 \mathrm{~V}$
What is potential developed for $\mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})} \rightarrow \mathrm{Ag}^{+1}(0.01 \mathrm{M})+\overline{\mathrm{e}}$ at 298 K ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+0.9184 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-0.9184 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $+0.6816 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-0.6816 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-0.6816 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given reaction is an oxidation reaction: $\mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})} \rightarrow \mathrm{Ag}^{+}_{(\mathrm{aq})} + \mathrm{e}^{-}$

The standard oxidation potential is the negative of the standard reduction potential:
$\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\text{ox}} = -\mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\text{red}} = -0.80 \mathrm{~V}$

According to the Nernst equation for the oxidation half-cell at $298 \mathrm{~K}$:
$\mathrm{E}_{\text{ox}} = \mathrm{E}^{\circ}_{\text{ox}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{n} \log_{10}[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]$

Here, the number of electrons transferred is $n = 1$ and the concentration is $[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}] = 0.01 \mathrm{~M} = 10^{-2} \mathrm{~M}$. Substituting these values:
$\mathrm{E}_{\text{ox}} = -0.80 - \dfrac{0.0592}{1} \log_{10}(10^{-2})$

$\mathrm{E}_{\text{ox}} = -0.80 - 0.0592 \times (-2)$

$\mathrm{E}_{\text{ox}} = -0.80 + 0.1184$

$\mathrm{E}_{\text{ox}} = -0.6816 \mathrm{~V}$

Answer: $-0.6816 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

---

### Q65. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If standard emf of cell
$\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{Zn}_{(1 \mathrm{M})}^{+2} \| \mathrm{Ag}_{(1 \mathrm{M})}^{+1}\right| \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})} \quad$ is 1.55 V.
What is electrical work done under standard conditions?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-144.750 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(B)** $-193.00 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(C)** $-299.150 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(D)** $-386.00 \mathrm{~kJ}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $-299.150 \mathrm{~kJ}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The cell reaction for the given cell is:

$\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})} + 2\mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+} \rightarrow \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{2+} + 2\mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

The number of moles of electrons transferred in the balanced cell reaction is $n = 2$.

Standard emf of the cell, $E^{\circ} = 1.55 \text{ V}$.

Faraday's constant, $F = 96500 \text{ C mol}^{-1}$.

The electrical work done under standard conditions is given by the standard Gibbs free energy change ($\Delta G^{\circ}$):

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -nFE^{\circ}$

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -2 \times 96500 \times 1.55$

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -299150 \text{ J}$

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -299.150 \text{ kJ}$

Answer: $-299.150 \mathrm{~kJ}$

</details>

---

### Q66. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\left(\mathrm{Mg}^{+2}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})} \mid \mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)=-2.37 \mathrm{~V}$.
What is potential for $\mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{s})} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{+2}(0.1 \mathrm{M})+2 \overline{\mathrm{e}}$ at 298 K ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+2.3996 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-2.3996 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $+2.3404 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-2.3404 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $+2.3996 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given standard reduction potential is $E^{\circ}_{\text{red}} = -2.37 \text{ V}$.

The given half-cell reaction is an oxidation reaction:
$\mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{s})} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{+2}(0.1 \mathrm{M}) + 2\mathrm{e}^{-}$

The standard oxidation potential is:
$E^{\circ}_{\text{ox}} = -E^{\circ}_{\text{red}} = -(-2.37 \text{ V}) = +2.37 \text{ V}$

Using the Nernst equation for the oxidation half-cell at $298 \text{ K}$:
$E_{\text{ox}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{ox}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{n} \log_{10}[\mathrm{Mg}^{+2}]$

Here, the number of electrons transferred is $n = 2$ and the concentration is $[\mathrm{Mg}^{+2}] = 0.1 \text{ M}$.

Substituting the values:
$E_{\text{ox}} = 2.37 - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10}(0.1)$

$E_{\text{ox}} = 2.37 - 0.0296 \times (-1)$

$E_{\text{ox}} = 2.37 + 0.0296 = +2.3996 \text{ V}$

Answer: $+2.3996 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

---

### Q67. MHT CET 2025 (21 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Select false statement from the following regarding electrochemical cell.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Standard cell potential of electro chemical cell is an intensive property.
- **(B)** Electrode potential depends on concentration of salt solution is in contact with electrode.
- **(C)** Standard free energy change of cell is an intensive property.
- **(D)** Electrical work done in galvanic cell is equal to decrease in Gibb's energy.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Standard free energy change of cell is an intensive property.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Standard cell potential ($E^{\circ}$) is an intensive property because it does not depend on the amount of substance present in the cell.

Electrode potential depends on the concentration of the electrolyte solution as given by the Nernst equation.

Standard free energy change ($\Delta G^{\circ}$) is an extensive property because it depends on the number of moles of electrons transferred ($n$) in the cell reaction, as given by the relation $\Delta G^{\circ} = -nFE^{\circ}$.

Electrical work done by a galvanic cell is equal to the decrease in Gibbs free energy, i.e., $W_{max} = -\Delta G$.

Thus, the statement that standard free energy change is an intensive property is false.

Answer: Standard free energy change of cell is an intensive property.

</details>

---

### Q68. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which from following statement is correct regarding emf of cell for the cell reaction,
$\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})}+\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
if concentration of $\mathrm{Cd}^{+2}$ is 10 times greater than concentration of $\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}$ at 298 K ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** greater than $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ by 0.0592 V
- **(B)** less than $E^{\circ}{ }_{\text {cell }}$ by 0.0592 V
- **(C)** greater than $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ by 0.0296 V
- **(D)** less than $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}{ }_{\text {cell }}$ by 0.0296 V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) less than $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}{ }_{\text {cell }}$ by 0.0296 V**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given cell reaction is:

$\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})} + \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} + \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

The number of electrons transferred in the reaction is $n = 2$.

According to the Nernst equation at $298 \text{ K}$:

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{n} \log_{10} \dfrac{[\mathrm{Cd}^{+2}]}{[\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}]}$

Given that the concentration of $\mathrm{Cd}^{+2}$ is 10 times greater than the concentration of $\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}$, we have $\dfrac{[\mathrm{Cd}^{+2}]}{[\mathrm{Cu}^{+2}]} = 10$.

Substituting the values into the equation:

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10}(10)$

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - 0.0296 \times 1$

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - 0.0296 \text{ V}$

Thus, the emf of the cell is less than $E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}}$ by $0.0296 \text{ V}$.

Answer: less than $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}{ }_{\text {cell }}$ by 0.0296 V

</details>

---

### Q69. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Assume the cell reaction,
$\mathrm{A}_{(\mathrm{s})}+\mathrm{B}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} \rightarrow \mathrm{~A}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+\mathrm{B}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
if $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}=-386 \mathrm{~kJ}$ at 298 K . What is $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1 V
- **(B)** 1.5 V
- **(C)** 2 V
- **(D)** $2.5 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 2 V**

**Detailed Solution:**

The relationship between standard Gibbs free energy change and standard cell potential is given by $\Delta G^{\circ} = -nFE_{\text{cell}}^{\circ}$.

For the given cell reaction, the number of electrons transferred is $n = 2$.

Given $\Delta G^{\circ} = -386 \text{ kJ} = -386000 \text{ J}$ and $F = 96500 \text{ C mol}^{-1}$.

Substituting the values into the equation:

$-386000 = -2 \times 96500 \times E_{\text{cell}}^{\circ}$

$-386000 = -193000 \times E_{\text{cell}}^{\circ}$

$E_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = \dfrac{-386000}{-193000} = 2 \text{ V}$

Answer: 2 V

</details>

---

### Q70. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which from following formulae is used to obtain value of $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^*$ for a reaction taking place in Dry cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{-\Delta G^{\circ}}{F}$
- **(B)** $\frac{-\Delta G^{\circ}}{2 F}$
- **(C)** $\frac{-\Delta G^{\circ}}{3 F}$
- **(D)** $\frac{-\Delta G^{\circ}}{4 F}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{-\Delta G^{\circ}}{2 F}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard Gibbs free energy change for a cell reaction is related to the standard cell potential by the equation:

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -n F E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}}$

For a dry cell (Leclanché cell), the half-reactions are:
Anode (oxidation): $Zn(s) \rightarrow Zn^{2+}(aq) + 2e^{-}$
Cathode (reduction): $2NH_4^{+}(aq) + 2MnO_2(s) + 2e^{-} \rightarrow Mn_2O_3(s) + 2NH_3(aq) + H_2O(l)$

The overall cell reaction involves the transfer of $2$ moles of electrons, which means $n = 2$.

Substituting $n = 2$ into the equation, we get:

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -2 F E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}}$

Rearranging the formula to solve for $E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}}$ gives:

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = \dfrac{-\Delta G^{\circ}}{2 F}$

Answer: $\frac{-\Delta G^{\circ}}{2 F}$

</details>

---

### Q71. MHT CET 2025 (20 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Standard potential $\left(\mathrm{E}^*\right)$ of $\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}{ }_{(a q)}+2 \overline{\mathrm{e}} \rightarrow \mathrm{Zn}_{(s)}$ is -0.76 V .
What is standard potential of reaction $2 \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Zn}^{+2}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+4 \overline{\mathrm{e}}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-1.52 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $+1.52 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.76 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $+0.76 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $+0.76 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given reaction is the reduction of zinc:

$\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}_{(aq)} + 2e^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Zn}_{(s)}$ with $E^{\circ} = -0.76 \text{ V}$

The required reaction is the oxidation of zinc:

$2\mathrm{Zn}_{(s)} \rightarrow 2\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}_{(aq)} + 4e^{-}$

This reaction is obtained by reversing the given reduction reaction and multiplying it by 2. Reversing a cell reaction changes the sign of its standard potential. Therefore, the standard oxidation potential is:

$E^{\circ}_{\text{ox}} = -E^{\circ}_{\text{red}} = -(-0.76 \text{ V}) = +0.76 \text{ V}$

Multiplying the reaction by a stoichiometric coefficient does not change the value of the standard potential because standard electrode potential is an intensive property. It depends only on the nature of the material and the concentration, not on the amount of substance reacting.

Thus, the standard potential for the given reaction remains $+0.76 \text{ V}$.

Answer: $+0.76 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

---

### Q72. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For the cell,
${ }^{\ominus} \mathrm{Zn}_{(s)}\left|\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}(1 \mathrm{M})\right|\left|\mathrm{Ag}^{+1}(1 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Ag}_{(s)}^{\oplus}$

If concentration of $\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}$ decreases to 0.1 M at 298 K . then emf of cell

**Options:**

- **(A)** increases by 0.0592 V
- **(B)** decreases by 0.0592 V
- **(C)** increases by 0.0296 V
- **(D)** decreases by 0.0296 V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) increases by 0.0296 V**

**Detailed Solution:**

The cell reaction is:

$\mathrm{Zn}_{(s)} + 2\mathrm{Ag}^{+}_{(aq)} \rightarrow \mathrm{Zn}^{2+}_{(aq)} + 2\mathrm{Ag}_{(s)}$

The number of electrons transferred is $n = 2$.

According to the Nernst equation at $298 \mathrm{K}$:

$E_{\mathrm{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10} \dfrac{[\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}]}{[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]^2}$

Initially, $[\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}] = 1 \mathrm{M}$ and $[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}] = 1 \mathrm{M}$.

$E_1 = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10} (1) = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}}$

When $[\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}]$ decreases to $0.1 \mathrm{M}$:

$E_2 = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10} \left(\dfrac{0.1}{1^2}\right)$

$E_2 = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10} (10^{-1})$

$E_2 = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} (-1)$

$E_2 = E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{cell}} + 0.0296 \mathrm{V}$

Change in emf $= E_2 - E_1 = 0.0296 \mathrm{V}$

Therefore, the emf of the cell increases by $0.0296 \mathrm{V}$.

Answer: increases by 0.0296 V

</details>

---

### Q73. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the number of faraday required to form $1 \mathrm{~mol~} \mathrm{H}_2$ by reduction of $\mathrm{H}^{+}$ions?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4$
- **(B)** $2$
- **(C)** $0.5$
- **(D)** $1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reduction reaction of $\text{H}^{+}$ ions to form hydrogen gas is given by:

$2\text{H}^{+} + 2\text{e}^{-} \rightarrow \text{H}_2$

From the stoichiometry of the reaction, $1$ mole of $\text{H}_2$ gas is produced by the consumption of $2$ moles of electrons.

Since the charge of $1$ mole of electrons is equal to $1$ Faraday ($1\text{ F}$), the charge required to produce $1$ mole of $\text{H}_2$ is $2$ Faradays.

Answer: $2$

</details>

---

### Q74. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For a Galvanic cell consisting zinc electrode and standard hydrogen electrode, $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}\left(\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2} \mid \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}\right)=-0.76 \mathrm{~V}$. Identify the reaction that takes place at positive electrode during working of cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Zn}^{+2}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{H}_{(\mathrm{g})}^{+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-}$
- **(D)** $2 \mathrm{H}^{+}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2 \mathrm{H}^{+}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard reduction potential of the zinc electrode is $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}/\mathrm{Zn}} = -0.76 \mathrm{~V}$ and that of the standard hydrogen electrode (SHE) is $E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{H}^{+}/\mathrm{H}_2} = 0.00 \mathrm{~V}$.

In a Galvanic cell, the electrode with the higher standard reduction potential acts as the cathode (positive electrode) and undergoes reduction. The electrode with the lower standard reduction potential acts as the anode (negative electrode) and undergoes oxidation.

Since $0.00 \mathrm{~V} > -0.76 \mathrm{~V}$, the standard hydrogen electrode acts as the cathode (positive electrode) and the zinc electrode acts as the anode (negative electrode).

At the positive electrode (cathode), reduction of hydrogen ions takes place:

$2\mathrm{H}^{+}_{(\mathrm{aq})} + 2\mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{g})}$

Answer: $2 \mathrm{H}^{+}{ }_{(\mathrm{aq})}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$

</details>

---

### Q75. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

How long should aqueous NaCl be electrolysed by passing 100 ampere current, so that 0.5 mol chlorine is released at anode?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 96500 seconds
- **(B)** 9650 seconds
- **(C)** 965 seconds
- **(D)** 96.5 seconds

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 965 seconds**

**Detailed Solution:**

The oxidation reaction at the anode is:

$2Cl^{-} \rightarrow Cl_2 + 2e^{-}$

From the stoichiometry of the reaction, 1 mole of $Cl_2$ gas is produced by the passage of 2 moles of electrons.

Therefore, 0.5 mole of $Cl_2$ gas requires $0.5 \times 2 = 1$ mole of electrons.

Charge required, $Q = 1 \text{ F} = 96500 \text{ C}$

Using the formula $Q = I \times t$:

$96500 = 100 \times t$

$t = \dfrac{96500}{100} = 965 \text{ seconds}$

Answer: 965 seconds

</details>

---

### Q76. MHT CET 2025 (19 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For the cell reaction,
$\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}+2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
Cell potential is less than $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ cell by 0.0592 V at 298 K when

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}\right]=1 \mathrm{M}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]=0.1 \mathrm{M}$
- **(B)** $\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}\right]=1 \mathrm{M}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]=0.01 \mathrm{M}$
- **(C)** $\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}\right]=0.1 \mathrm{M}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]=1 \mathrm{M}$
- **(D)** $\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}\right]=0.01 \mathrm{M}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]=1 \mathrm{M}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}\right]=1 \mathrm{M}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]=0.1 \mathrm{M}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given cell reaction is:

$\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}+2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

The number of electrons transferred in the reaction is $n = 2$.

According to the Nernst equation at 298 K:

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{n} \log_{10} \dfrac{[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}]}{[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]^2}$

It is given that the cell potential is less than $E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}}$ by 0.0592 V, which means:

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - 0.0592$

Substituting this into the Nernst equation:

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - 0.0592 = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10} \dfrac{[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}]}{[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]^2}$

$0.0592 = \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10} \dfrac{[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}]}{[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]^2}$

$\log_{10} \dfrac{[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}]}{[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]^2} = 2$

$\dfrac{[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}]}{[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]^2} = 10^2 = 100$

Checking the given options, for $[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}] = 1 \mathrm{M}$ and $[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}] = 0.1 \mathrm{M}$:

$\dfrac{[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}]}{[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]^2} = \dfrac{1}{(0.1)^2} = \dfrac{1}{0.01} = 100$

This satisfies the required condition.

Answer: $\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{+2}\right]=1 \mathrm{M}$ and $\left[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\right]=0.1 \mathrm{M}$

</details>

---

### Q77. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which from following equations represents a correct relationship between standard cell potential and equilibrium constant for cell reaction?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{\circ}=-\frac{2.303 \mathrm{RT}}{\mathrm{nF}}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{nF}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \ln \mathrm{K}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The relationship between standard Gibbs free energy change and standard cell potential is given by:

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -nFE_{\text{cell}}^{\circ}$

The relationship between standard Gibbs free energy change and equilibrium constant is:

$\Delta G^{\circ} = -2.303 RT \log_{10} K$

Equating the two expressions for $\Delta G^{\circ}$:

$-nFE_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = -2.303 RT \log_{10} K$

$E_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = \dfrac{2.303 RT}{nF} \log_{10} K$

At standard temperature $T = 298 \text{ K}$, substituting $R = 8.314 \text{ J K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}$ and $F = 96500 \text{ C mol}^{-1}$:

$\dfrac{2.303 \times 8.314 \times 298}{96500} \approx 0.0592 \text{ V}$

Therefore, the equation becomes:

$E_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = \dfrac{0.0592}{n} \log_{10} K$

Answer: $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$

</details>

---

### Q78. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the number of faraday required to produce $\cdot 0.18 \mathrm{~g}$ aluminium at cathode during electrolysis of molten $\mathrm{AlCl}_3$ ?
(Molar mass of $\mathrm{Al}=27 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.02 F
- **(B)** 0.03 F
- **(C)** 0.25 F
- **(D)** 0.30 F

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 0.02 F**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reduction reaction of aluminium at the cathode is given by:

$\mathrm{Al}^{3+} + 3\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Al}$

This indicates that $3 \mathrm{~F}$ of electricity is required to deposit $1 \mathrm{~mol}$ of $\mathrm{Al}$ ($27 \mathrm{~g}$).

Number of moles of $\mathrm{Al}$ to be deposited $= \dfrac{0.18}{27} = \dfrac{2}{300} \mathrm{~mol}$

Number of faradays required $= 3 \times \text{moles of } \mathrm{Al}$

$= 3 \times \dfrac{2}{300} = 0.02 \mathrm{~F}$

Answer: 0.02 F

</details>

---

### Q79. MHT CET 2024 (16 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Consider the following cell
$\left.\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})} \mid \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{2+}\right)\left|\mathrm{KCl}_{(\mathrm{sat})}\right| \underset{\text { (paste) }}{\mathrm{Hg}_2 \mathrm{Cl}_2} \mid \mathrm{Hg}$
$\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ cell $=1.007 \mathrm{~V}$ and $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ calomel $=0.242 \mathrm{~V}$ What is the standard potential of Zn ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad-0.765 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** 0.765 V
- **(C)** -1.247 V
- **(D)** 1.247 V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\quad-0.765 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard cell potential is given by the difference between the standard reduction potentials of the cathode and the anode.

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cathode}} - E^{\circ}_{\text{anode}}$

From the given cell representation, zinc acts as the anode (oxidation half-cell) and the calomel electrode acts as the cathode (reduction half-cell).

$E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{calomel}} - E^{\circ}_{\text{Zn}}$

Substituting the given values:

$1.007 = 0.242 - E^{\circ}_{\text{Zn}}$

$E^{\circ}_{\text{Zn}} = 0.242 - 1.007$

$E^{\circ}_{\text{Zn}} = -0.765 \text{ V}$

Answer: $\quad-0.765 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

---

### Q80. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the decreasing order of deposition of metal on electrode if standard reduction potentials are given as -
$\mathrm{Ag}^{+}\left|\mathrm{Ag}=0.80 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{Cu}^{2+}\right| \mathrm{Cu}=0.337 \mathrm{~V}$
$\mathrm{Sn}^{2+}\left|\mathrm{Sn}=-0.136 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{Cd}^{2+}\right| \mathrm{Cd}=-0.403 \mathrm{~V}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Ag} \gt \mathrm{Cu} \gt \mathrm{Sn} \gt \mathrm{Cd}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Cu} \gt \mathrm{Sn} \gt \mathrm{Cd} \gt \mathrm{Ag}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Sn} \gt \mathrm{Cd} \gt \mathrm{Ag} \gt \mathrm{Cu}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Cd} \gt \mathrm{Sn} \gt \mathrm{Cu} \gt \mathrm{Ag}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Ag} \gt \mathrm{Cu} \gt \mathrm{Sn} \gt \mathrm{Cd}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The tendency of a metal ion to undergo reduction and deposit on the electrode is directly proportional to its standard reduction potential ($E^{\circ}$).

Higher the standard reduction potential, greater is the tendency of the metal ion to get reduced and deposited.

The given standard reduction potentials are:
$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Ag}^{+}/\mathrm{Ag}} = 0.80 \mathrm{~V}$
$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}/\mathrm{Cu}} = 0.337 \mathrm{~V}$
$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Sn}^{2+}/\mathrm{Sn}} = -0.136 \mathrm{~V}$
$E^{\circ}_{\mathrm{Cd}^{2+}/\mathrm{Cd}} = -0.403 \mathrm{~V}$

Arranging the standard reduction potentials in decreasing order:
$0.80 \mathrm{~V} \gt 0.337 \mathrm{~V} \gt -0.136 \mathrm{~V} \gt -0.403 \mathrm{~V}$

Therefore, the decreasing order of deposition of the metals is $\mathrm{Ag} \gt \mathrm{Cu} \gt \mathrm{Sn} \gt \mathrm{Cd}$.

Answer: $\mathrm{Ag} \gt \mathrm{Cu} \gt \mathrm{Sn} \gt \mathrm{Cd}$

</details>

---

### Q81. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the mass of ' Ca ' deposited at cathode by passing 0.8 ampere current through molten $\mathrm{CaCl}_2$ in 60 minutes.
[Molar mass of $\mathrm{Ca}=40 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.4 g
- **(B)** 0.5 g
- **(C)** 0.6 g
- **(D)** 0.7 g

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 0.6 g**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reaction at the cathode is:

$\mathrm{Ca}^{2+} + 2\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Ca}$

The number of moles of electrons required to deposit one mole of Ca is $n = 2$.

The given parameters are $I = 0.8 \mathrm{~A}$, $t = 60 \mathrm{~min} = 3600 \mathrm{~s}$, and $M = 40 \mathrm{~g~mol}^{-1}$.

Using Faraday's first law of electrolysis, the mass of Ca deposited is:

$W = \dfrac{I \times t \times M}{n \times F}$

Substituting the values:

$W = \dfrac{0.8 \times 3600 \times 40}{2 \times 96500}$

$W = \dfrac{115200}{193000}$

$W = 0.5968 \mathrm{~g} \approx 0.6 \mathrm{~g}$

Answer: 0.6 g

</details>

---

### Q82. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ of $\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{++}\right|\left|\mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{+}\right| \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ is 0.647 volt. Calculate the $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ}$ if $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}_{\mathrm{u}}^{\circ}}$ is 0.153 V .

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.8 V
- **(B)** 0.5 V
- **(C)** -0.8 V
- **(D)** -0.5 V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 0.8 V**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the given cell representation, copper ($\mathrm{Cu}$) undergoes oxidation and acts as the anode, while silver ($\mathrm{Ag}$) undergoes reduction and acts as the cathode.

The standard cell potential is given by:

$\mathrm{E}_{\text{cell}}^{\circ} = \mathrm{E}_{\text{cathode}}^{\circ} - \mathrm{E}_{\text{anode}}^{\circ}$

Substituting the given values:

$0.647 = \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ} - \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}}^{\circ}$

$0.647 = \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ} - 0.153$

$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ} = 0.647 + 0.153$

$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ} = 0.8 \text{ V}$

Answer: 0.8 V

</details>

---

### Q83. MHT CET 2024 (15 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Find the number of electrons that generate 1 coulomb charge ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad 6.24 \times 10^{18}$
- **(B)** $1.25 \times 10^{18}$
- **(C)** $6.22 \times 10^{23}$
- **(D)** $3.12 \times 10^{18}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\quad 6.24 \times 10^{18}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The charge of a single electron is $e = 1.602 \times 10^{-19}$ C.

Let $n$ be the number of electrons required to generate a total charge $q = 1$ C.

Using the quantization of charge, $q = ne$

$n = \dfrac{q}{e}$

$n = \dfrac{1}{1.602 \times 10^{-19}}$

$n = 6.24 \times 10^{18}$

Answer: $\quad 6.24 \times 10^{18}$

</details>

---

### Q84. MHT CET 2024 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

When fused NaCl undergoes electrolysis the product formed at cathode is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Na}-\mathrm{Hg}_{(1)}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

In the electrolysis of fused (molten) $\mathrm{NaCl}$, the only ions present are $\mathrm{Na}^{+}$ and $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$.

At the cathode (negative electrode), reduction of sodium ions takes place:
$\mathrm{Na}^{+} + \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

At the anode (positive electrode), oxidation of chloride ions takes place:
$2\mathrm{Cl}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{g})} + 2\mathrm{e}^{-}$

Therefore, sodium metal $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ is formed at the cathode.

Answer: $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

</details>

---

### Q85. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the $\text{E}^\circ_{\text{cell}}$ of $\text{Al} \vert{} \text{Al}_2(\text{SO}_4)_{3(1\text{M})} \vert{}\vert{} \text{HCl}_{(1\text{M})} \vert{} \text{H}_{2(\text{g})(1\text{atm})}, \text{Pt}$ (Given $\text{E}^\circ_{\text{Al}^{3+}\vert{}\text{Al}}$ $= -1.66 \text{ V}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1.66 V
- **(B)** -1.66 V
- **(C)** 0.0533 V
- **(D)** 1.14 V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 1.66 V**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given cell representation is $\text{Al} \vert{} \text{Al}^{3+} \vert{}\vert{} \text{H}^+ \vert{} \text{H}_2, \text{Pt}$.

The anode is the aluminum electrode and the cathode is the Standard Hydrogen Electrode (SHE).

The standard reduction potential of the cathode is $\text{E}^\circ_{\text{H}^+\vert{}\text{H}_2} = 0.00 \text{ V}$.

The standard reduction potential of the anode is $\text{E}^\circ_{\text{Al}^{3+}\vert{}\text{Al}} = -1.66 \text{ V}$.

The standard cell potential is given by:

$\text{E}^\circ_{\text{cell}} = \text{E}^\circ_{\text{cathode}} - \text{E}^\circ_{\text{anode}}$

$\text{E}^\circ_{\text{cell}} = 0.00 \text{ V} - (-1.66 \text{ V})$

$\text{E}^\circ_{\text{cell}} = 1.66 \text{ V}$

Answer: 1.66 V

</details>

---

### Q86. MHT CET 2024 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which of the following is released at cathode during electrolysis of aqueous sodium chloride?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $\mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

During the electrolysis of aqueous sodium chloride, the ions present in the solution are $\mathrm{Na}^+$, $\mathrm{Cl}^-$, $\mathrm{H}^+$, and $\mathrm{OH}^-$.

At the cathode, reduction takes place. The two possible reduction reactions are:
$\mathrm{Na}^+_{(aq)} + e^- \rightarrow \mathrm{Na}_{(s)}$ ($E^\circ = -2.71$ V)
$2\mathrm{H}_2\mathrm{O}_{(l)} + 2e^- \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_{2(g)} + 2\mathrm{OH}^-_{(aq)}$ ($E^\circ = -0.83$ V)

Since the standard reduction potential of water is higher than that of sodium ions, water is preferentially reduced at the cathode. Thus, hydrogen gas ($\mathrm{H}_{2(g)}$) is released at the cathode.

At the anode, oxidation of chloride ions takes place due to the overpotential of oxygen, releasing chlorine gas ($\mathrm{Cl}_{2(g)}$).

Answer: $\mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$

</details>

---

### Q87. MHT CET 2024 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the quantity of electricity required to produce 4.8 g of Mg (molar mass $=24 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ) from its salt solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** 10 F
- **(B)** 4 F
- **(C)** 1 F
- **(D)** 0.4 F

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 0.4 F**

**Detailed Solution:**

Moles of Mg produced = $\dfrac{4.8}{24} = 0.2$ mol

The reduction reaction for magnesium is:

$Mg^{2+} + 2e^{-} \rightarrow Mg$

From the stoichiometry, 1 mole of Mg requires 2 moles of electrons, which corresponds to 2 F of electricity.

Quantity of electricity required for 0.2 mol of Mg = $0.2 \times 2$ F = 0.4 F

Answer: 0.4 F

</details>

---

### Q88. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ cell for $\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{2+} \square \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{+}\right| \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ is 1.2 V . What is the emf of the cell at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\quad-1.2 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** 2.4 V
- **(C)** -2.4 V
- **(D)** 1.2 V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 1.2 V**

**Detailed Solution:**

The cell reaction is $\text{Cd}_{(s)} + 2\text{Ag}^{+}_{(aq)} \rightarrow \text{Cd}^{2+}_{(aq)} + 2\text{Ag}_{(s)}$.

The Nernst equation for the cell at $25^{\circ}\text{C}$ is given by:

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0591}{n} \log \dfrac{[\text{Cd}^{2+}]}{[\text{Ag}^{+}]^2}$

Given that $[\text{Cd}^{2+}] = 1\text{ M}$ and $[\text{Ag}^{+}] = 1\text{ M}$, and $E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} = 1.2\text{ V}$.

Substituting the values into the Nernst equation:

$E_{\text{cell}} = 1.2 - \dfrac{0.0591}{2} \log \dfrac{1}{1^2}$

$E_{\text{cell}} = 1.2 - \dfrac{0.0591}{2} \log (1)$

Since $\log(1) = 0$:

$E_{\text{cell}} = 1.2 - 0 = 1.2\text{ V}$

Answer: 1.2 V

</details>

---

### Q89. MHT CET 2024 (04 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the amount of electricity required in coulombs to convert 0.08 mol of $\mathrm{MnO}_4^{-}$to $\mathrm{Mn}^{2+}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 96500 C
- **(B)** 38600 C
- **(C)** 48250 C
- **(D)** 19300 C

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 38600 C**

**Detailed Solution:**

The balanced half-reaction for the reduction of $\mathrm{MnO}_4^{-}$ to $\mathrm{Mn}^{2+}$ is:

$\mathrm{MnO}_4^{-} + 8\mathrm{H}^{+} + 5\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Mn}^{2+} + 4\mathrm{H}_2\mathrm{O}$

The oxidation state of $\mathrm{Mn}$ changes from $+7$ to $+2$, which involves a transfer of $5$ electrons.

Number of moles of electrons required for $1$ mole of $\mathrm{MnO}_4^{-}$ is $5$ moles.

For $0.08$ moles of $\mathrm{MnO}_4^{-}$, the number of moles of electrons required is $0.08 \times 5 = 0.4$ moles.

Since $1$ mole of electrons carries a charge of $1$ Faraday ($96500$ C), the total charge required is:

$Q = 0.4 \times 96500$ C

$Q = 38600$ C

Answer: 38600 C

</details>

---

### Q90. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the quantity of electricity required to liberate 0.1 mole of chlorine gas during electrolysis of molten sodium chloride.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 9665 C
- **(B)** 19300 C
- **(C)** 14500 C
- **(D)** 96500 C

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 19300 C**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reaction at the anode during the electrolysis of molten sodium chloride is:

$2Cl^{-} \rightarrow Cl_{2} + 2e^{-}$

From the stoichiometry of the reaction, $1$ mole of $Cl_{2}$ gas is liberated by $2$ moles of electrons.

Therefore, $0.1$ mole of $Cl_{2}$ gas will require $0.1 \times 2 = 0.2$ moles of electrons.

The quantity of electricity required for $1$ mole of electrons is $1$ Faraday ($1$ F $= 96500$ C).

Quantity of electricity required $= 0.2 \times 96500$ C $= 19300$ C.

Answer: 19300 C

</details>

---

### Q91. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which from following is NOT true about electrolysis of molten NaCl ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Cl}_2$ gas is liberated at anode.
- **(B)** Na is deposited at cathode.
- **(C)** The decomposition of NaCl into $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ and $\mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$ is spontaneous.
- **(D)** Electrical energy is used to carry out the reaction.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) The decomposition of NaCl into $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ and $\mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$ is spontaneous.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Electrolysis of molten NaCl is a non-spontaneous process that requires the input of electrical energy to drive the chemical reaction.

At the cathode (reduction): $\mathrm{Na}^{+} + \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Na}_{(l)}$

At the anode (oxidation): $2\mathrm{Cl}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Cl}_{2(g)} + 2\mathrm{e}^{-}$

Thus, $\mathrm{Cl}_2$ gas is liberated at the anode and sodium metal is deposited at the cathode. Since electrical energy is used to carry out the reaction, the decomposition of NaCl into Na and $\mathrm{Cl}_2$ is non-spontaneous.

Therefore, the statement that the decomposition is spontaneous is incorrect.

Answer: The decomposition of NaCl into $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ and $\mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$ is spontaneous.

</details>

---

### Q92. MHT CET 2024 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which from following expressions is used to calculate $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}$ for the following cell at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ ?
$\mathrm{Pb}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{Pb}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{++}\right|\left|\mathrm{Ag}_{(10 \mathrm{M})}^{+}\right| \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\cdot \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\left(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}+0.0592\right) \mathrm{V}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\left(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}-0.0592\right) \mathrm{V}$
- **(C)** $E_{\text {cell }}=\left(E_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}-0.0296\right) V$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}=\left(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{\circ}+0.0296\right) \mathrm{V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\cdot \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\left(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}+0.0592\right) \mathrm{V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The cell reaction for the given cell is:

$\mathrm{Pb}_{(\mathrm{s})} + 2\mathrm{Ag}^{+}_{(\mathrm{aq})} \rightarrow \mathrm{Pb}^{2+}_{(\mathrm{aq})} + 2\mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$

The number of electrons exchanged in the reaction is $n = 2$.

Using the Nernst equation at $25^{\circ}\mathrm{C}$:

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{n} \log_{10} \dfrac{[\mathrm{Pb}^{2+}]}{[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}]^2}$

Substituting the given concentrations $[\mathrm{Pb}^{2+}] = 1\mathrm{M}$ and $[\mathrm{Ag}^{+}] = 10\mathrm{M}$:

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - \dfrac{0.0592}{2} \log_{10} \dfrac{1}{(10)^2}$

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - 0.0296 \log_{10} (10^{-2})$

$E_{\text{cell}} = E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} - 0.0296 \times (-2)$

$E_{\text{cell}} = (E^{\circ}_{\text{cell}} + 0.0592) \mathrm{V}$

Answer: $\cdot \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\left(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}+0.0592\right) \mathrm{V}$

</details>

---

### Q93. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Calculate } \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ} \text { for } \mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{++}\right|\left|\mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{MM})}^{+}\right| \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})} . \\ & {\left[\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cd}}^{\circ}=-0.403 \mathrm{~V} ; \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ}=0.799 \mathrm{~V}\right]}\end{aligned}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.202 V$
- **(B)** $-1.202 V$
- **(C)** $0.396 V$
- **(D)** $-0.396 V$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $1.202 V$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the given cell, anode is $\mathrm{Cd}$ and cathode is Ag.
$\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ} & =\mathrm{E}_{\text {cathode }}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{\text {anode }}^{\circ} \\
& =0.799-(-0.403) \\
& =1.202 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q95. MHT CET 2023 (14 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which from following expressions is used to find the cell potential of $\mathrm{Cd}_{\text {(s) }}\left|\mathrm{Cd}_{\text {(aq) }}^{++}\right|\left|\mathrm{Cu}_{\text {(aq) }}^{++}\right| \mathrm{Cu}_{\text {(s) }}$ cell at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}-0.0296 \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cd}^{++}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{++}\right]}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}+0.0296 \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cd}^{++}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{++}\right]}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}-0.0592 \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{++}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cd}^{++}\right]}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}+0.0592 \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{++}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cd}^{++}\right]}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}-0.0296 \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cd}^{++}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{++}\right]}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Cell reaction:
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \quad \text { (oxidation at anode) } \\
& \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})} \quad \text { (reduction at cathode) } \\
& \hline \mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})}+\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{2+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})} \\
& \text { (overall reaction) }
\end{aligned}
$$
Now,
$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }} & =\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\mathrm{o}}-\frac{0.0592 \mathrm{~V}}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \frac{[\text { Product }]}{[\text { Reactant }]} \\
& =0.02-\frac{0.0592 \mathrm{~V}}{2} \log _{10} \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cd}^{++}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{++}\right]} \\
& =\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\prime}-0.0296 \log \frac{\left[\mathrm{Cd}^{++}\right]}{\left[\mathrm{Cu}^{++}\right]}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q96. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ for the following.
$\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{Zn}_{(1 \mathrm{M})}^{++}\right|\left|\mathrm{Pb}_{(1 \mathrm{M})}^{++}\right| \mathrm{Pb}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ if $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Zn}^{\circ}}^{\circ}=-0.763 \mathrm{~V}$ and
$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Pb}}^{\mathrm{o}}=-0.126 \mathrm{~V}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.637 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-0.530 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.889 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $0.789 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.637 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the given cell, $\mathrm{Zn}$ acts as anode and $\mathrm{Pb}$ acts as cathode.
$\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cathode }}^0-\mathrm{E}_{\text {anode }}^0$
$=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Pb}}^0-\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Zn}}^0=-0.126 \mathrm{~V}-(-0.763) \mathrm{V}$
$=0.637 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q97. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate the time required in second to deposit $6.35 \mathrm{~g}$ copper from its salt solution by passing 5 ampere current.
[Molar mass of $\mathrm{Cu}=63.5 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** 3600
- **(B)** 3700
- **(C)** 3860
- **(D)** 4000

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) 3860**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})} \\ & \text { Mole ratio }=\frac{1 \mathrm{~mol}}{2 \mathrm{~mole}^{-}} \\ & \mathrm{W}=\frac{\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{A}) \times \mathrm{t}(\mathrm{s})}{96500\left(\mathrm{C} / \mathrm{mol} \mathrm{e}^{-}\right)} \times \text {mole ratio } \times \text { molar mass } \\ & 6.35 \mathrm{~g}=\frac{5 \times \mathrm{t}}{96500\left(\mathrm{C} / \mathrm{mol} \mathrm{e}^{-}\right)} \times \frac{1 \mathrm{~mol}}{2 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{e}} \times 63.5 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\ & \mathrm{t}=\frac{6.35 \times 96500 \times 2}{5 \times 63.5}=3860 \text { seconds }\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q98. MHT CET 2023 (13 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ if the equilibrium constant for following reaction is $1.2 \times 10^6$.
$$
2 \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{++}+\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}
$$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.36 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-0.36 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.18 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $0.18 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.36 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K} \text { at } 298 \mathrm{~K} \\ & \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{mq})}^{+}+\mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})} \\ & \frac{\mathrm{Cu}_{\text {(aq) }}^{+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{\text {(aq) }}^{2+}+\mathrm{e}^{-}}{2 \mathrm{Cu}_{\text {(aq) }}^{+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{(\text {aq) }}^{2+}+\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}} \\ & \therefore \quad \mathrm{n}=1 \\ & \therefore \quad \mathrm{E}_{\text {eell }}^0=\frac{0.0592}{1} \log _{10}\left(1.2 \times 10^6\right) \\ & =0.0592\left(\log 1.2+\log 10^6\right) \\ & =0.0592(0.079+6) \\ & =0.0592 \times 6.079 \\ & =0.36 \mathrm{~V} \\ & \end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q99. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}$ for $\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{Zn}_{(0.1 \mathrm{M})}^{++}\right|\left|\mathrm{Cr}_{(0.1 \mathrm{M})}^{+++}\right| \mathrm{Cr}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ if $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ is $0.02 \mathrm{~V}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-0.05 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $0.03 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.06 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $0.07 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.03 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reaction is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& 3 \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}+2 \mathrm{Cr}_{(0.1 \mathrm{M})}^{3+} \longrightarrow 3 \mathrm{Zn}_{(0.1)}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{Cr}_{(\mathrm{s})} \\
& \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}-\frac{0.0592 \mathrm{~V}}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \frac{\left[\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}\right]^3}{\left[\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}\right]^2}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\begin{aligned} \therefore \quad \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }} & =0.02 \mathrm{~V}-\frac{0.0592 \mathrm{~V}}{6} \log _{10} \frac{(0.1)^3}{(0.1)^2} \\ & =0.02 \mathrm{~V}-\frac{0.0592 \mathrm{~V}}{6} \log _{10} 0.1 \\ & =0.02 \mathrm{~V}+\frac{0.0592 \mathrm{~V}}{6} \times 1 \\ & =0.02 \mathrm{~V}+0.0099 \mathrm{~V} \\ & =0.03 \mathrm{~V}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q101. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Electrolytic cells containing $\mathrm{Zn}$ and $\mathrm{Al}$ salt solutions are connected in series. If $6.5 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Zn}$ is deposited in one cell calculate mass of Al deposited in second cell (molar mass : $\mathrm{Zn}=65$, $\mathrm{Al}=27$ ) by passing definite quantity of electricity?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.4 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(B)** $2.1 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(C)** $2.7 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(D)** $1.8 \mathrm{~g}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $1.8 \mathrm{~g}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Cell 1:
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{m})}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})} \\
& (\text { mole ratio) })_1=\frac{1 \mathrm{~mol}}{2 \mathrm{~mole}^{-}}
\end{aligned}
$$
Cell 2:
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{sq})}^{3+}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})} \\
& (\text { mole ratio })_2=\frac{1 \mathrm{~mol}}{3 \mathrm{~mole}^{-}}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\begin{aligned} & \frac{\mathrm{W}_1}{(\text { mole ratio })_1 \times \mathrm{M}_1}=\frac{\mathrm{W}_2}{(\text { mole ratio })_2 \times \mathrm{M}_2} \\ & \frac{6.5 \mathrm{~g}}{1 \mathrm{~mol} / 2 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{e}^{-} \times 65 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}} \\ & =\frac{\mathrm{W}_2}{1 \mathrm{~mol} / 3 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{e}^{-} \times 27 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}} \\ & \frac{6.5 \mathrm{~g} \times 2}{65}=\frac{\mathrm{W}_2 \times 3}{27} \\ & \mathrm{~W}_2=\frac{6.5 \times 2 \times 27}{65 \times 3}=1.8 \mathrm{~g}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q102. MHT CET 2023 (12 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}$ for the reaction $\mathrm{Mg}_{(s)}+\mathrm{Sn}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{++} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{mq})}^{++}+\mathrm{Sn}_{(s)}$ if $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0$ is $2.23 \mathrm{~V}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-430.4 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(B)** $215.2 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(C)** $645.6 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(D)** $-860.8 \mathrm{~kJ}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $-430.4 \mathrm{~kJ}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ} & =-\mathrm{nFE}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ} \\ & =-2 \times 96500 \times 2.23 \\ & =-430390 \mathrm{~J} \\ & =-430.4 \mathrm{~kJ}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q103. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate the $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^o$ for $\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{Zn}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{+}\right|\left|\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{+}\right| \mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\left[\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Zn}}^{\circ}=-0.763 \mathrm{~V} ; \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cd}}^{\circ}=-0.403 \mathrm{~V}\right]$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.36 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $1.17 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.36 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-1.17 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.36 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the given cell reaction, anode is $\mathrm{Zn}$ and cathode is $\mathrm{Cd}$.
$\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^o & =\mathrm{E}_{\text {cathode }}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{\text {anode }}^0 \\
& =-0.403-(-0.763) \\
& =0.36 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q104. MHT CET 2023 (11 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Find the number of faradays of electricity required to produce $45 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Al}$ from molten $\mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(B)** $3 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(C)** $5\mathrm{~F}$
- **(D)** $7 \mathrm{~F}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5\mathrm{~F}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\mathrm{Al}^{3+}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Al}
$$

The equation shows that 3 moles of electrons are required to produce 1 mole of $\mathrm{Al}$ (i.e., $27 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Al}$ ).
$\therefore \quad 3 \mathrm{~F}$ of electricity is required to produce $27 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Al}$ from molten $\mathrm{Al}_2 \mathrm{O}_3$.
$\therefore \quad$ Faradays of electricity required to produce $45 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Al}=\frac{3}{27} \times 45=5 \mathrm{~F}$

</details>

---

### Q105. MHT CET 2023 (10 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ in which following reaction occurs. $\mathrm{Mg}_{(\mathrm{s})}+2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(1 \mathrm{M})}^{+} \rightarrow \mathrm{Mg}_{(1 \mathrm{M})}^{++}+2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ if $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ}=0.8 \mathrm{~V}$ and $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Mg}}^{\circ}=-2.37 \mathrm{~V}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-3.17 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $3.17 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-1.57 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $1.57 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3.17 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For the given cell reaction, anode is $\mathrm{Mg}$ and cathode is Ag.
$\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0 & =\mathrm{E}_{\text {cathode }}^0-\mathrm{E}_{\text {anode }}^0 \\
& =0.8-(-2.37) \\
& =3.17 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q106. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A reaction, $\mathrm{Ni}_{(\mathrm{s})}+\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{++} \rightarrow \mathrm{Ni}_{(\mathrm{IM} 2}^{++}+\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ occurs in a cell. Calculate $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^6$ if $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}}^0=0.337 \mathrm{~V}$ and $\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ni}}^{\mathrm{o}}=-0.257 \mathrm{~V}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.594 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-0.594 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.08 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $0.08 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.594 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard cell potential is given by
$\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^o & =\mathrm{E}_{\text {cathode }}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{\text {anode }}^o \\
\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ} & =\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ni}}^{\circ} \\
& =(0.337 \mathrm{~V})-(-0.257 \mathrm{~V}) \\
& =0.337 \mathrm{~V}+0.257 \mathrm{~V} \\
& =0.594 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q107. MHT CET 2023 (09 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Calculate $\Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ}$ for the cell:
$\mathrm{Sn}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\operatorname{Sn}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{++}\right|\left|\operatorname{Ag}_{(\mathrm{IM})}^{+}\right| \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})} \text { at } 25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\left(\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=0.90 \mathrm{~V}\right)$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $-173.7 \mathrm{~kJ}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \Delta \mathrm{G}^{\circ} & =-\mathrm{nF}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ} \\ & =-2 \times 96500 \times 0.90 \\ & =-173700 \mathrm{~J} \\ & =-173.7 \mathrm{~kJ}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q108. MHT CET 2022 (11 Aug Shift 1)

### Q111. MHT CET 2022 (10 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

How many Faraday of electricity is required to produce $5 \mathrm{~g}$ of magnesium from magnesium chloride?

- **(A)** $2.451 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(B)** $0.417 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(C)** $6.0 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(D)** $9.634 \mathrm{~F}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{Mg}^{+2}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{Mg} \\ & 2 \mathrm{~mol} \quad 1 \mathrm{~mol} \\ & \frac{5}{24}=0.208 \mathrm{~mol} \mathrm{Mg} \\ & \therefore \text { Mol of charge required or faraday of change required } \\ & =0.208 \times 2 \\ & =0.416 \mathrm{~F} \\ & \end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q112. MHT CET 2022 (08 Aug Shift 2)

$W_2=0.635 \mathrm{~g}$

### Q117. MHT CET 2022 (06 Aug Shift 1)

What is the change in potential of following cell $\mathrm{Zn}(\mathrm{s})\left|\begin{array}{c}\mathrm{Zn} \mathrm{n}^{2+} \\ 1 \mathrm{M}\end{array}\right| \begin{gathered}\mathrm{Pb}^{2+} \\ 1 \mathrm{M}\end{gathered} \mid \mathrm{Pb}(\mathrm{s})$ if concentration of ions at anode is increased 10 times?

### Q121. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Calculate standard cell potential for a cell having following reaction,
$\begin{aligned} & 2 \mathrm{Al}_{(\mathrm{s})}+3 \mathrm{Ni}^{2+} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{Al}^{3+}+3 \mathrm{Ni}(\mathrm{s}) \\ & \left(\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ni}}^{\circ}=-0.25 \mathrm{~V}, \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{A} 1}^{\circ}=-1.66 \mathrm{~V}\right)\end{aligned}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.50 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $1.41 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.50 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $0.41 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.41 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ni}}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ai}}^{\circ} \\ & =-0.25-(-1.66) \\ & =1.41 \mathrm{~V}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q123. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which from following equation is correct for relation between strandard cell potential and equilibrium constant?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=\log _{10} \mathrm{~K} \frac{\mathrm{n}}{0.0592}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\log _{10} \mathrm{~K} \frac{\mathrm{n}}{0.0592}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

We know $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0-\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{Q}$
Or at equilibrium $0=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0-\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$
Or $\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^0=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$

</details>

### Q124. MHT CET 2022 (05 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is standard reduction potential of $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+} \mid \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ if $\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ of following cell is 0.46 ?
$\mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}\left|\mathrm{cu}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{2+} \| \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}\right| \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})} ; \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}^{+} / \mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ}=0.80 \mathrm{v} \mid$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.56 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $0.34 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $1.44 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $1.26 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.34 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{\circ}-\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cu}}^{\circ} \\ & 0.46=0.80-\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{\circ} \\ & \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cu}}^{\circ}=0.34 \mathrm{v}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q125. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

What is the charge required for the reduction of two moles of $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}$ to $\mathrm{Cu}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.89 \times 10^5 \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $1.93 \times 10^5 \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $9.65 \times 10^5 \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $3.86\times 10^5 \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $3.86\times 10^5 \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{Cu}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu} \\ & 1 \mathrm{~mole} \quad 2 \mathrm{~mole}^{-} \\ & 2 \mathrm{~mole} \quad 4 \mathrm{~mole}^{-} \\ & \text {Charge of } 1 \mathrm{~mole} \text { electrons }=96500 \mathrm{C} \\ & \text { Charge required for the reduction of two moles of } \mathrm{Cu}^{2+} \\ & =4 \times 96500=3.86 \times 10^5 \mathrm{C}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q126. MHT CET 2021 (24 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which is the weight of A1 deposited at cathode when 1 ampere current is passed through molten $\mathrm{AlCl}_3$ for 9650 seconds?
(At mass of $\mathrm{Al}=27$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 3.0 g
- **(B)** 9.0 g
- **(C)** 13.6 g
- **(D)** 0.9 g

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 0.9 g**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \text { Number of equivalents of } \mathrm{Al} \text { deposited }=\frac{\mathrm{I} . \mathrm{t}}{\mathrm{F}} \\ & =\frac{1 \times 9650}{96500}=0.1 \\ & \text { Number. Of equivalents }=\text { moles } \times \mathrm{n}_{\text {factor }} \\ & \stackrel{+3}{\mathrm{AlCl}_3 \longrightarrow \mathrm{Al}_{\text {factor }}=3} \\ & 0.1=\text { moles } \times 3 \\ & \text { moles of } \mathrm{Al}=0.1 / 3 \\ & \text { mass of } \mathrm{Al}=\frac{0.1}{3} \times 27=0.9 \mathrm{~g}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q127. MHT CET 2021 (23 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

How many electrons flow through the wire if a current of 1.5 ampere flow through it for 3 hours?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.60 \times 10^{19}$
- **(B)** $ 1.01 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $10.1 \times 10^{19}$
- **(D)** $1.60 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $ 1.01 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{Q}=\mathrm{ne}=\mathrm{It} \\ & \mathrm{n} \times 1.6 \times 10^{-19}=1.5 \times 3 \times 60 \times 60 \\ & \mathrm{n}=\text { no. of electrons } \\ & \mathrm{e}=\text { charge of one electron } \\ & \mathrm{n}=\frac{1.5 \times 3 \times 60 \times 60}{1.6 \times 10^{-19}}=1.01 \times 10^{23}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q130. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Pure dihydrogen (99.5\%) is obtained by the electrolysis of

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{NaOH}_{(\mathrm{aq})}$ using $\mathrm{Zn}$ electrode
- **(B)** pure water
- **(C)** dil. $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{SO}_4$ using cadmium electrodes
- **(D)** warm barium hydroxide using $\mathrm{Ni}$ electrodes

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) warm barium hydroxide using $\mathrm{Ni}$ electrodes**

**Detailed Solution:**

- In the laboratory, high purity compounds can be obtained only with electrolysis.
- $\quad$ Similarly, highly purified dihydrogen is also obtained by electrolyzing water using a small amount of acid or base.
- Here, $\mathrm{Ba}(\mathrm{OH})_2$ is a base. Hence, electrolyzing water in the presence of a base $\mathrm{Ba}(\mathrm{OH})_2$ gives us the highly purified (> $99.95 \%$ ) Dihydrogen $\left(\mathrm{H}_2\right)$ using Ni electrodes.

</details>

### Q131. MHT CET 2021 (22 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

How many Faraday of electricity is required to deposit $0.8 \mathrm{~g}$ of calcium at cathode by the electrolysis of $\mathrm{CaCl}_2$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(B)** $0.04 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(C)** $2.5 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(D)** $2 \mathrm{~F}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.04 \mathrm{~F}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{Ca}^{2+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Ca}_{(\mathrm{s})} \\
& \qquad \mathrm{nf}=2 \\
& \text { moles of calcium }=\frac{0.8}{40}=2 \times 10^{-2} \\
& \text { no. of equivalents of calcium } \\
& =\text { moles } \times \mathrm{nf} \\
& =2 \times 10^{-2} \times 2 \\
& =4 \times 10^{-2}=0.04
\end{aligned}
$$
To deposit 1 equivalent of electrolyte 1 Faraday electricity is required.

To deposit 0.04 equivalents of electrolyte, $0.04 \mathrm{~F}$ electricity is required.

</details>

### Q132. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the number of moles of electrons passed when current of 5 ampere is passed through a solution of $\mathrm{FeCl}_3$ for 20 minutes?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.25 \times 10^{-2}$
- **(B)** $1.56 \times 10^{-2}$
- **(C)** $3.12 \times 10^{-2}$
- **(D)** $4.25 \times 10^{-2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $6.25 \times 10^{-2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& Q=I . t \\
& =5 \times 20 \times 60 \\
& =6000 \mathrm{C}
\end{aligned}
$$
Charge of 1 mole electrons $=96500 \mathrm{C}$
no. of moles of electrons $=\frac{\mathrm{Q}}{96500}=\frac{6000}{96500} \simeq 6.25 \times 10^{-2}$

</details>

---

### Q133. MHT CET 2021 (21 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the number of electrons passed through an electrolyte solution when 1 ampere current is passed for 16.1 minutes?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $5.022 \times 10^{24}$
- **(B)** $3.011 \times 10^{22}$
- **(C)** $6.022 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $2.022 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $6.022 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{q}=\mathrm{ne} \\
& \mathrm{q}=\mathrm{I} . \mathrm{t} \\
& \mathrm{n}=\text { no. of electrons } \\
& \mathrm{e}=\text { change of one electron } \\
& \mathrm{I}=\text { Current supplied } \\
& \mathrm{t}=\text { time in seconds } \\
& \mathrm{q}=1 \times 16.1 \times 60=\mathrm{ne} \\
& \mathrm{n}=\frac{16.1 \times 60}{1.602 \times 10^{-19}}=6.022 \times 10^{21}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q135. MHT CET 2021 (20 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

How many faraday of electricity is required to produce $10 \mathrm{~g}$ of calcium metal (molar mass $=40 \mathrm{~g} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ ) from calcium ions?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.5 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(B)** $2.0\mathrm{~F}$
- **(C)** $0.50 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(D)** $1.0 \mathrm{~F}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.50 \mathrm{~F}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
2 \mathrm{e}^{-}+\mathrm{Ca}^{2+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Ca}_{(\mathrm{s})}
$$
Charge of $1 \mathrm{~mol}$ electron $=1$ faraday of electricity To produce $1 \mathrm{~mol}$ calcium metal $2 \mathrm{~F}$ electricity is required Moles of calcium $=\frac{10}{40}=0.25$
To produce $0.25 \mathrm{~mol}$ calcium electricity required $=2 \times 0.25=0.5 \mathrm{~F}$

</details>

### Q136. MHT CET 2020 (20 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

$\mathrm{E}^{\circ}$ cell is $1 \cdot 049 \mathrm{~V}$ and involves transfer of 2 electrons, calculate equilibrium constant of cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.75 \times 10^{35}$
- **(B)** $2.75 \times 10^{10}$
- **(C)** $0 \cdot 524 \times 10^{35}$
- **(D)** $2 \cdot 098 \times 10^{10}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $2.75 \times 10^{35}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{0}=1.049 \mathrm{~V}, \quad \mathrm{n}=2, \quad \mathrm{~K}=?$
$\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{0}=\frac{0.0592}{\mathrm{n}} \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}$
$\therefore \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}=\frac{\mathrm{E}_{\text {cell }}^{0} \times \mathrm{n}}{0.0592}=\frac{1.049 \times 2}{0.0592}$
$\therefore \log _{10} \mathrm{~K}=35.439$
$\therefore \mathrm{K} =$ Antilog $(35.439)$
$\therefore \mathrm{K}=2.75 \times 10^{35}$

</details>
