### Q138. MHT CET 2020 (19 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

How many faradays of electricity is required to produce $4.8 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Mg}$ at cathode in the electrolysis of molten $\mathrm{MgCl}_{2} ?$ (Molar mass of $\mathrm{Mg}=24 \mathrm{~g} / \mathrm{mol}$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0.4 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(B)** $4 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(C)** $10 \mathrm{~F}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{~F}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $0.4 \mathrm{~F}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(A)
$\mathrm{Mg}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Mg}$ (at cathode)
$24 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Mg}=2 \mathrm{~F}$ of electricity
$\therefore 4.8 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Mg}=\frac{4.8 \times 2}{24}=0.4 \mathrm{~F}$ of electricity.

</details>

### Q140. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A solution of $\mathrm{CuSO}_{4}$ is electrolysed using a current of $1 \cdot 5$ amperes for 10 minutes. What mass of Cu is deposited at cathode ? (At. mass of $\mathrm{Cu}=63 \cdot 7$ )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.395g
- **(B)** 0.150g
- **(C)** 0.637g
- **(D)** 0.297g

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 0.297g**

**Detailed Solution:**

(D)
The half reaction at cathode is
$\mathrm{Cu}_{\text {(aq) }}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{e}^{--} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cu}_{(\mathrm{s})}$ Mole ratio $=\frac{1}{2}, \mathrm{t}=10 \mathrm{~min}=600 \mathrm{~s}$
Mass of $\mathrm{Cu}$ deposited $=\frac{\mathrm{I} \times \mathrm{t}}{96500} \times$ mole ratio $\times$ molar mass of $\mathrm{Cu}$
$=\frac{1.5 \times 600}{96500} \times \frac{1}{2} \times 63.7=0.297 \mathrm{~g}$

</details>

### Q141. MHT CET 2020 (16 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

How many electrons are involved in the reaction when $0 \cdot 40$ F of electricity is passed through an electrolytic solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.642 \times 10^{25}$
- **(B)** $2.4088 \times 10^{23}$
- **(C)** $1.505 \times 10^{24}$
- **(D)** $6.022 \times 10^{23}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2.4088 \times 10^{23}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(C)
$\begin{array}{l}
1 \mathrm{~F}=96500 \mathrm{C} \\
\therefore \quad 0.4 \mathrm{~F}=96500 \times 0.4=38600 \mathrm{C}
\end{array}$
$\begin{aligned}
\text { Now, } 96500 \mathrm{C} &=6.022 \times 10^{23} \text { electrons } \\
\therefore 38600 \mathrm{C} &=\frac{6.022 \times 10^{23} \times 38600}{96500} \\
&=2.4088 \times 10^{23} \text { electrons }
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q142. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the standard free energy change for the cell, having following cell reaction? $2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{aq} .)}^{+}+\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})} \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}+\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{2+}, \mathrm{E}^{\circ} \mathrm{cell}=1 \cdot 20 \mathrm{~V}$

- **(A)** $-231 \cdot 6 \mathrm{~kJ}$

- **(D)** $-260 \cdot 8 \mathrm{K} \mathrm{j}$

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

$2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{s})} \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Ag}_{(\mathrm{s})}+\mathrm{Cd}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{2+}$
$\therefore \mathrm{n}=2$

### Q143. MHT CET 2020 (15 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

For the following cell, standard potential of copper electrode in $0.337 \mathrm{~V}$ and standard cell potential is $0.463 \mathrm{~V}$ $\mathrm{Cu}\left|\mathrm{Cu}^{2+}(1 \mathrm{M}) \| \mathrm{Ag}^{+}(1 \mathrm{M})\right| \mathrm{Ag}$
What is the standard potential of silver electrode?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-0.126 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $0.800 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-0.463 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $0.126 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $0.800 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{0}=0.463 \mathrm{~V}, \quad \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}}^{0}=0.337 \mathrm{~V}$
$\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{0}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{0}-\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}}^{0}$
$\therefore \mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ag}}^{0}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{cell}}^{0}+\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}}^{0}=0.463+0.337=0.800 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q144. MHT CET 2020 (14 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

How many electrons flow when a current of 5 amp is passed through a cell for 200 sec?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $9.65 \times 10^{21}$
- **(B)** $1 \cdot 60 \times 10^{21}$
- **(C)** $3 \cdot 12 \times 10^{21}$
- **(D)** $6 \cdot 24 \times 10^{21}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $6 \cdot 24 \times 10^{21}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\mathrm{Q}=\mathrm{I} \times \mathrm{t}=5 \times 200=1000 \mathrm{C}
$$
The charge of one electron is $1.602 \times 10^{-19} \mathrm{C}$
No. of electrons passed $=\frac{1000 \mathrm{C}}{1.602 \times 10^{-19} \mathrm{C}}=6.24 \times 10^{21}$

</details>

### Q145. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

How much charge in coulombs is required for the reduction of one mole of $\mathrm{Al}^{3+}$ to Al?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.930 \times 10^{4} \mathrm{C}$
- **(B)** $2 \cdot 895 \times 10^{5} \mathrm{C}$
- **(C)** $2 \cdot 895 \times 10^{4} \mathrm{C}$
- **(D)** $1.930 \times 10^{5} \mathrm{C}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $2 \cdot 895 \times 10^{5} \mathrm{C}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Al}^{3 *}+3 \mathrm{e}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Al}$
1 mole of electron $=1 \mathrm{~F}=96500$ coulombs
$\therefore 3 \mathrm{e}^{-}=3 \mathrm{~F}=3 \times 96500=2,89,500=2.895 \times 10^{5}$ coulombs

</details>

### Q146. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

What is the standard potential of cell $\mathrm{Ni}\left|\mathrm{Ni}^{2+}{ }_{(1 \mathrm{M})} \| \mathrm{Cu}^{2+}{ }_{(1 \mathrm{M})}\right| \mathrm{Cu}$ If $\mathrm{E}^{\circ} \mathrm{Cu}=0 \cdot 337 \mathrm{~V}$ and $\mathrm{E}^{\circ} \mathrm{Ni}=-0 \cdot 236 \mathrm{~V}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0 \cdot 101 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-0 \cdot 136 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $0.573 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-0.753 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $0.573 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} \mathrm{E}_{\text {ceil }}^{0} &=\mathrm{E}_{\text {cuthode }}^{0}-\mathrm{E}_{\text {anode }}^{0}=\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Cu}}^{0}-\mathrm{E}_{\mathrm{Ni}_{\mathrm{i}}}^{0} \\ &=0.337 \mathrm{~V}-(-0.236 \mathrm{~V})=0.573 \mathrm{~V} \end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q147. MHT CET 2020 (13 Oct Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

During the electrolysis of aqueous sodium chloride the product obtained at anode is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{Na}_{(\mathrm{s})}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{O}_{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{Cl}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

During the electrolysis of aqueous $\mathrm{NaCl}$,
i. $\mathrm{H}_{2}$ is liberated at cathode
ii. $\mathrm{Cl}_{2}$ gas is released at anode

</details>

### Q148. MHT CET 2020 (12 Oct Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which of the following statements is true for electrolytic cell?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Oxidation occurs at positive electrode
- **(B)** Oxidation occurs at negative electrode
- **(C)** Anode is negative and cathode is positive electrode
- **(D)** Reduction occurs at positive electrode

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Oxidation occurs at positive electrode**

**Detailed Solution:**

The anode of electrolytic cell is positive on which oxidation takes place.

### Q149. MHT CET 2019 (02 May Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Two electrolytic cells are connected in series containing <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math> solution and molten <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>.</mo></math> If in electrolysis <math><mn>0.4</mn></math> moles of ‘ <math><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></math> ’ are deposited on cathode of ﬁrst cell. The number of moles of ‘ <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></math> ’ deposited on cathode of the second cell is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>0.6</mn></math> moles
- **(B)** <math><mn>0.27</mn></math> moles
- **(C)** <math><mn>0.18</mn></math> moles
- **(D)** <math><mn>0.4</mn></math> moles

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>0.4</mn></math> moles**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to Faraday’s second law of electrolysis: 
<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>63.5</mn></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>63.5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>27</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>7.2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>  
<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <em>No. of moles of</em> <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7.2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>27</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0.27</mn></math>  
<em>Hint:</em> <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> <em>and</em> <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q150. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Standard hydrogen electrode (SHE) is a

**Options:**

- **(A)** Primary reference electrode
- **(B)** Secondary reference electrode
- **(C)** Metal – sparingly soluble salt electrode
- **(D)** Metal – metal ion electrode

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Primary reference electrode**

**Detailed Solution:**

Standard hydrogen electrode (SHE) is primary reference electrode. SHE is represented by <math><mi>P</mi><mi>t</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><msup><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> is assigned a zero potential at all temperatures corresponding to reaction. 
<math><msup><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>(</mo><mi>g</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

</details>

### Q151. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The correct representation of Nernst’s equation for half- cell reaction <math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.0592</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.0592</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.0592</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.0592</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.0592</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<strong>Key Idea</strong> General Nernst equation for a reaction is given as. 
<math><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.0591</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>X</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfrac><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>]</mo></mrow><mrow><mo>[</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>]</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>  
For the half – cell 
<math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>  
The correct Nernst’s equation is 
<math><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.0591</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q152. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The SI unit of electrochemical equivalent is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>J</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>K</mi><mi>g</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>K</mi><mi>g</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi>m</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>K</mi><mi>g</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mi>K</mi><mi>g</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The electrochemical equivalent of a substance is the mass liberated in electrolysis by 1 coulomb. It can be calculated by assuming by 1 coulomb Faraday’s second law and using the experimental fact that the equivalent weight in gams of all substances is liberate by 96500 C. 
Electrochemical equivalent <math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mtext> </mtext><mi mathvariant="normal">w</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>96500</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>  
Gm per coulomb. Its unit is <math><mi>k</mi><mi>g</mi><mtext> </mtext><msup><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>.</mo></math>

</details>

### Q153. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Calculate E.M.F. of following cell art 298 K Zn(S) <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>Z</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>S</mi><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0.01</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>u</mi><mi>S</mi><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1.0</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mi>C</mi><mi>u</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> if 
<math><msup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2.0</mn><mi>V</mi></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** 2.0 V
- **(B)** 2.0592 V
- **(C)** 2.0296
- **(D)** 1.0508 V

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 2.0592 V**

**Detailed Solution:**

<strong>Key Idea</strong> General from of Nernst equation is 
<math><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>m</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>R</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>I</mi><mi>n</mi><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>M</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>  
<strong>Oxidation</strong> <math><mi>Z</mi><mi>n</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mi>Z</mi><msup><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mtext> </mtext><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mn>0.01</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>  
<strong>Reduction</strong> <math><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>u</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1.0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>M</mi><mo>→</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>u</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>  
Using Nernst equation, 
<math><msub><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>c</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>l</mi><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.059</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>0.59</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0.059</mn></math>  
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>2.059</mn><mi>V</mi></math>

</details>

### Q154. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

In the electrolysis of aqueous sodium chloride with inert electrodes the products obtained at anode and cathode respectively are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi></math> and <math><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

In the electrolysis of aqueous sodium chloride with inert electrodes, the products obtained at anode and cathode respectively are <math><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> and <math><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>.</mo></math>  
The solution contains four ions <math><mi>N</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>O</mi><mi>H</mi><mo>.</mo></math>  
Following reaction takes place: 
<strong>At cathode</strong> 
<math><mtable><mtr><mtd><mrow><maligngroup></maligngroup><mi>N</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>;</mo></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mrow><maligngroup></maligngroup><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2.71</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mrow><maligngroup></maligngroup><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></mover><mi>H</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>;</mo></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mrow><maligngroup></maligngroup><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi><mo>/</mo><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0.83</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>  
<strong>At anode</strong> 
<math><mtable><mtr><mtd><mrow><maligngroup></maligngroup><mn>2</mn><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1.36</mn><mi>V</mi></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mrow><maligngroup></maligngroup><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mi>O</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mrow><maligngroup></maligngroup><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi><mo>/</mo><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1.23</mn><mtext> </mtext><mi>V</mi></mrow></mtd></mtr></mtable></math>  
<math><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi><mo>/</mo><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>N</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>.</mo></math> is formed at cathode. 
Whereas <math><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi><mo>/</mo><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>&gt;</mo><msubsup><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>/</mo><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msubsup><mo>.</mo></math> So, formed at anode.

</details>

### Q155. MHT CET 2019 (Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Which among the following is correct for electrolysis of brine solution?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> gas is liberated at cathode
- **(B)** Sodium metal is collected at anode
- **(C)** <math><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> gas is liberated at cathode
- **(D)** <math><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> gas is liberated at cathode

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> gas is liberated at cathode**

**Detailed Solution:**

In electrolysis of brine solution, <math><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> gas is liberated at cathode. The solution contains four ions <math><mi>N</mi><msup><mrow><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><msup><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math> and <math><mover><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow></mover><mi>H</mi></math> ions. There occurs a race amongst them for their discharge at their respective electrodes. 
Following electrode reactions are possible: 
<math><mi mathvariant="bold">C</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">a</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">t</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">h</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">o</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">d</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">e</mi><mtext> </mtext><mn>2</mn><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mi>O</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>O</mi></mrow><mo>-</mo></mover><mi>H</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>  
<math><mi mathvariant="bold">A</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">n</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">o</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">d</mi><mi mathvariant="bold">e</mi><mtext>         </mtext><mn>2</mn><mi>C</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>a</mi><mi>q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>→</mo><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>  
In this electrolysis, <math><msub><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> at cathode and <math><mi>C</mi><msub><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub></math> at anode are given off.

</details>

### Q156. MHT CET 2017

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Which among the following equations represents the reduction taking place in lead accumulator at positive electrode, While it is being used as a source of electrical energy?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mtext>P</mtext><mtext>b</mtext><mo>→</mo><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext> </mtext></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext> </mtext></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext> </mtext><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>→</mo><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mtext> </mtext><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reaction at positive electrode is,
<math> <mrow> <mtext>P</mtext><mtext>b</mtext><msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>s</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup> <mtext>H</mtext> <mo>+</mo> </msup> <mo>+</mo><mtext>S</mtext><msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <msup> <mrow></mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn><mo>−</mo> </mrow> </msup> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>a</mtext><mtext>q</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup> <mtext>e</mtext> <mo>−</mo> </msup> <mo>→</mo><mtext>P</mtext><mtext>b</mtext><mtext>S</mtext><msub> <mtext>O</mtext> <mn>4</mn> </msub> <mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>s</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mtext>O</mtext><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mtext>l</mtext><mo stretchy="false">)</mo> </mrow> </math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext> </mtext><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mtext> </mtext></math> is converted into <math><mtext>P</mtext><msup><mrow><mtext>b</mtext></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q157. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

In a dry cell, which element acts as negative electrode?

**Options:**

- **(A)** Zinc
- **(B)** Graphite
- **(C)** Ammonium chloride
- **(D)** Manganese dioxide

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Zinc**

**Detailed Solution:**

In a dry cell, zinc acts as negative electrode.

</details>

### Q158. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In the cell represented <math><mi>P</mi><msub><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msub><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi>M</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfenced><mi>A</mi><msub><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msub></math>, the reducing agent is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>P</mi><mi>b</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mi>P</mi><msup><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>A</mi><mi>g</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi>A</mi><msup><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mi>P</mi><mi>b</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi>P</mi><msub><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msub><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><msubsup><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi>m</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mo>+</mo></mrow></msubsup></mrow></mfenced><mi>A</mi><msub><mrow><mi>g</mi></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>s</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow></msub></math>
The element losing <math><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math> is called the reducing agent.
<math><mi>P</mi><mi>b</mi><mo>⟶</mo><mi>P</mi><msup><mrow><mi>b</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

### Q159. MHT CET 2016

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

How many faraday of electricity are required to deposit 10 g of calcium from molten calcium chloride using insert electrodes? (Molar mass of calcium <math><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mi> </mi><mi>g</mi><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> )

**Options:**

- **(A)** 0.5 F
- **(B)** 1 F
- **(C)** 0.25 F
- **(D)** 2 F

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 0.5 F**

**Detailed Solution:**

2 moles of electrons are required to deposit 1 mole of calcium
Mass of calcium deposited <math><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mi> </mi><mi>g</mi></math>
Molar mass of calcium <math><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><mi> </mi><mi>g</mi><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>40</mn><mi>g</mi><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><msup><mrow><mi>l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mi> </mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.25</mn><mi> </mi><mi>m</mi><mi>o</mi><mi>l</mi></math>
2F are required for 1 mole of calcium
'x' F are required for 0.25 mole of calcium
<math><mo>∴</mo><mi> </mi><mi> </mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.25</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn><mi> </mi></math>F

</details>

### Q160. MHT CET 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The standard reduction potential for $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+} / \mathrm{Fe}$ and $\mathrm{Sn}^{2+} / \mathrm{Sn}$ electrodes are $-0.44$ and $-0.14 \mathrm{~V}$ respectively. For the cell reaction, $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}+\mathrm{Sn} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Fe}+\mathrm{Sn}^{2+}$ the standard emf is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+0.30 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-0.58 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $+0.58 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-0.30 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-0.30 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}+\mathrm{Sn} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Fe}+\mathrm{Sn}^{2+}$
$$
=-0.44+(0.14)
$$
$=-0.30 \mathrm{~V}$

</details>

### Q161. MHT CET 2011

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

According to Faraday's first law

**Options:**

- **(A)** $w=\frac{96500 \times E}{I \times t}$
- **(B)** $w=\frac{E \times I \times t}{96500}$
- **(C)** $E=\frac{I \times t \times 96500}{w}$
- **(D)** $E=\frac{I \times w}{t \times 96500}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $w=\frac{E \times I \times t}{96500}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

According to Faraday's first law,
$$
\begin{array}{l}
w=Z I t \\
Z=\frac{E}{96500} \\
w=\frac{E I t}{96500}
\end{array}
$$
where, $I=$ current
$$
\begin{array}{l}
t=\text { time } \\
E=\text { equivalent weight } \\
w=\text { weight deposited }
\end{array}
$$

</details>

### Q162. MHT CET 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Oxidation potential of unimoles of calomel is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+0.25 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $0.00 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $+0.287 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $-0.28 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-0.28 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Calomel is used as a secondary reference electrode. Since $\mathrm{Hg}$ is less reactive than $\mathrm{H}$, its standard reduction potential is positive. Hence, its standard oxidation potential must be negative, $i e,-0.28 \mathrm{~V}$. [As standard reduction potential = $-$ standard oxidation potential.]

</details>

### Q163. MHT CET 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Given, for $\mathrm{Sn}^{4+} / \mathrm{Sn}^{2+}$, standard reduction potential is $0.15 \mathrm{~V}$ and for $\mathrm{Au}^{3+} / \mathrm{Au}$, standard reduction potential is $1.5 \mathrm{~V}$. For the reaction, $3 \mathrm{Sn}^{2+}+2 \mathrm{Au}^{3+} \longrightarrow 3 \mathrm{Sn}^{4+}+2 \mathrm{Au}$
the value of $E_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ is,

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+1.35$
- **(B)** $+2.55$
- **(C)** $-1.35$
- **(D)** $-2.55$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $+1.35$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
E_{\text {cell }}^{\circ} &=E_{\text {cathode }}^{\circ}-E_{\text {anode }}^{\circ} \\
&=1.5-0.15=1.35 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

1 C electricity deposits

- **(C)** electrochemical equivalent of Ag
- **(D)** half of electrochemical equivalent of Ag

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) electrochemical equivalent of Ag**

From Faraday's first law,
mass, $w=Z \cdot Q$
when,
$$
Q=1 \mathrm{C}
$$
$w=Z=$ electrochemical equivalent.

### Q165. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

When 10 A current is passed for 80 min, the volume of hydrogen gas liberated is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $11.14 \mathrm{~L}$
- **(B)** $5.57 \mathrm{~L}$
- **(C)** $22.4 \mathrm{~L}$
- **(D)** $2.78 \mathrm{~L}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $5.57 \mathrm{~L}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{H}^{+}+\underset{1 \mathrm{~F}}{e^{-}} \longrightarrow \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{H}_{2}$
At STP,
$\because 96500 \mathrm{C}$ liberates $=11.2 \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{H}_{2}$
$\therefore 10 \times 80 \times 60 \mathrm{C}$ will liberate
$$
\begin{aligned} &=\frac{11.2 \times 10 \times 80 \times 60}{96500} \mathrm{~L} \mathrm{H}_{2} \\=& 5.57 \mathrm{~L} \text { hydrogen gas } \end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q166. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The number of electrons required to reduce $4.5 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Al}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.03 \times 10^{18}$
- **(B)** $3.01 \times 10^{18}$
- **(C)** $4.95 \times 10^{26}$
- **(D)** $7.31 \times 10^{20}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $3.01 \times 10^{18}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\underset{27}{\mathrm{Al}^{3+}}+3 e^{-} \longrightarrow \underset{27 \mathrm{~g}}{\mathrm{Al}}$
$27 \mathrm{~g}$ of $\mathrm{Al}$ is reduced $\mathrm{by}=3 \times 6.023 \times 10^{23} e^{-} \mathrm{s}$
$4.5 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~g}$ of Al will be reduced by
$$
\begin{array}{l}
=\frac{3 \times 6.023 \times 10^{23} \times 4.5 \times 10^{-5}}{27} \\
=3.01 \times 10^{18} \text { electrons }
\end{array}
$$

</details>

### Q167. MHT CET 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The standard reduction potential for $\mathrm{Mg}^{2+} / \mathrm{Mg}$ is $-2.37 \mathrm{~V}$ and for $\mathrm{Cu}^{2+} / \mathrm{Cu}$ is $0.337$. The $E_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}$ for the following reaction is $\mathrm{Mg}+\mathrm{Cu}^{2+} \longrightarrow \mathrm{Mg}^{2+}+\mathrm{Cu}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+2.03 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(B)** $-2.03 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(C)** $-2.7 \mathrm{~V}$
- **(D)** $+2.7 \mathrm{~V}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $+2.7 \mathrm{~V}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$E_{\text {cell }}^{\circ}=E_{\text {cathode }}^{\circ}-E_{\text {anode }}^{\circ}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
=E_{\left(\mathrm{Cu}^{2+} / \mathrm{Cu}\right)}^{\circ}-E_{\left(\mathrm{Mg}^{2+} / \mathrm{Mg}\right)}^{\circ} &=0.337-(-2.37) \mathrm{V} \\
&=2.7 \mathrm{~V}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q168. MHT CET 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The standard reduction potentials at $298 \mathrm{~K}$ for the following half-cell reactions are given on next page
$\mathrm{Zn}^{2+}(a q)+2 e^{-} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{Zn}(s)-0.762$
$\mathrm{Cr}^{3+}(a q)+3 e^{-} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{Cr}(s)-0.74$
$2 \mathrm{H}^{+}(a q)+2 e^{-} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{H}_{2}(g)+0.00$
$\mathrm{Fe}^{3+}(a q)+e^{-} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{Fe}^{2+}(a q)+0.77$
Which one of the following is the strongest reducing agent?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{Zn}(s)$
- **(B)** $\operatorname{Cr}(s)$
- **(C)** $\mathrm{H}_{2}(s)$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{Fe}^{2+}(a q)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\mathrm{Zn}(s)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The metals having higher negative value of standard reduction potential are placed above hydrogen in electrochemical series. The metals placed above hydrogen has a great tendency to donate electrons or oxidising power. The metals having great oxidising power are strongest reducing agent. Zn has higher negative value of standard reduction potential. Therefore, it is the strongest reducing agent.

</details>

### Q170. MHT CET 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Cell reaction is spontaneous, when

**Options:**

- **(A)** $E_{\text {red }}^{\circ}$ is negative
- **(B)** $E_{\mathrm{red}}^{\circ}$ is positive
- **(C)** $\Delta G^{\circ}$ is negative
- **(D)** $\Delta G^{\circ}$ is positive

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\Delta G^{\circ}$ is negative**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\Delta G=\Delta H-T \Delta S$
For a spontaneous celll reaction, $\Delta H$ should be negative and $\Delta S$ should be positive. Hence, $\Delta G$ should be negative.

</details>

### Q171. MHT CET 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The standard $E_{\text {red }}^{\circ}$ values of $A, B$ and $C$ are $+0.68 \mathrm{~V},-2.54 \mathrm{~V},-0.50 \mathrm{~V}$ respectively. The
order of their reducing power is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $A>B>C$
- **(B)** $A>C>B$
- **(C)** $C>B>A$
- **(D)** $B>C>A$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $B>C>A$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Reducing character is based upon higher negative value of reduction electrode potential.
Thus, order of reducing character is
$$
B>C>A
$$

</details>

### Q172. MHT CET 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

EMF of hydrogen electrode in term of $\mathrm{pH}$ is (at 1 atm pressure).

**Options:**

- **(A)** $E_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}=\frac{R T}{F} \times \mathrm{pH}$
- **(B)** $E_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}=\frac{R T}{F} \cdot \frac{1}{\mathrm{pH}}$
- **(C)** $E_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}=\frac{2.303 \mathrm{RT}}{F} \mathrm{pH}$
- **(D)** $E_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}=-0.591 \mathrm{pH}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $E_{\mathrm{H}_{2}}=-0.591 \mathrm{pH}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$2 \mathrm{H}^{+}+2 e^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}_{2}$
According to Nernst equation,
$$
\begin{aligned}
E &=E^{\circ}+\frac{0.0591}{n} \log \frac{1}{\left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]^{2}} \\
E &=0-\frac{0.0591}{2} \log \left[\mathrm{H}^{+}\right]^{2} \\
&=-0.0591 \mathrm{pH}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q173. MHT CET 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The amount of silver deposited on passing $2 \mathrm{~F}$ of electricity through aqueous solution of $\mathrm{AgNO}_{3}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $54 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(B)** $108 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(C)** $216 \mathrm{~g}$
- **(D)** $324 \mathrm{~g}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $216 \mathrm{~g}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

According Faraday law's
$$
\begin{array}{l}
m=z \times Q=\frac{E \times Q}{96500} \\
m=\frac{108}{96500} \times 2 \times 96500=216 \mathrm{~g}
\end{array}
$$

</details>
